LOCKSTEP HOT JOIN: A PLAYER JOINS A RUNNING SESSION
1 November 2026 | branch contrib/ferrite-hotjoin (from smoke d9c299dd) | offline: no X4 launched, nothing live measured

SUMMARY
- Built, default off, in three stages that can each be tested on their own:
  1. Save/load equality. src/native/engine/lockstep_join.h writes, at the step boundary of an agreed tick T, the
     join save and a join package (the side table plus the gate state) on every running Windows client. A joiner
     loads the save with the snapshot boot and, at its admission, restores what X4's loader rebuilt differently: the
     AI timed-wake tree (every blocked Wait's deadline and every other timed wake, in the session's order), both AI
     wake vectors, the MD scheduler heap (every pending cue check and delay, with its tie-breaking sequence number)
     and the MD sequence counter, the session's portable identity of every imported object, the game and real
     clock bits, the runtime-ID allocator head, the session tick T (the joiner counts session ticks), the probe's
     own session state (Lua providers) and, from package version 2 (J1 j1-11, 1.6), the world side table: every
     destructible object's hull and shield group state and every static component's pose, which X4's save rounds or
     leaves to the loader's clock; from version 3 (J1 j1-12, 1.7) every flying ship's navigation state by bank role,
     every object's attention level and due, the shield groups' other census fields and the gate's first-call tables;
     from version 4 (J1 j1-14, 1.8) the pathing manager's agent batches, cursors and new-agent list (then one wake of the
     pathing thread so that the pass of T+1 runs as the session's) and the hazard regions' damage field pass times.
  2. The join protocol. tools/dev/native_gate_relay.py --join with absent slots: the relay stands in for a slot nobody
     plays, asks every present client to save at T through a JOIN record in that slot's input blob, collects the
     savers' SAVED records (same portable digest from every saver, else refused), answers the joiner's launcher,
     replays the session's own rows to the joiner at its own speed, checks every replayed tick against the session,
     announces a handover tick H in the input stream and from H on treats the slot as a normal client.
  3. Fail loudly at every stage: hashes of the save, the package and each of its sections; a digest of the
     client-independent content that every saver must agree on; a strict restore that stops the joiner on any
     difference; a per-tick check of the joiner's clocks and declared digest against the session's during the whole
     catch-up; refusal (never a silent desync) when a save fails, savers disagree, the joiner diverges, stalls or would
     make the session wait longer than allowed.
- Offline proof: the package is byte for byte the same in C and Python (golden SHA-256 shared by both tests); capture
  and restore run against a synthetic AI director and MD scheduler with the engine's layouts; every pinned engine
  site is checked on the real X4 9.00 executable with tamper refusals; the relay protocol runs end to end with
  simulated clients for protocols 3 and 4, including divergence, slow joiners, failed and disagreeing saves.
- Not shown yet (the live tests J1-J3 decide it): that the restored world equals the running one. The side table
  covers what this repository already knows the loader re-rolls (AI Waits, MD cue delays) and their generalisation
  (all timed wakes, the whole MD heap) and, from version 3 (1.7), attention levels and dues, navigation banks and deadlines
  and the gate's first-call tables; Gravidar cursors, listener order and the loaded objects' runtime IDs are not covered
  (section 8). Every armed module that is not classified is listed in the
  package; strict mode refuses a save while one is armed, report mode (J1) saves and marks the package diagnostic.
- Pause and catch-up: the Windows save is a pause of the whole session for the length of the save (every client
  saves at T; estimated 5-20 s for the census world, measured by J1 as save_us). The joiner then has to close
  gap = (save + copy + load) x tick rate, about 2,100 ticks at 30 Hz with a 70 s gap. Measured X4 step costs leave an
  unpaced joiner 0-13 steps/s faster than a 30 Hz session, so in practice the relay ends the catch-up with a
  bounded final pause at H (expected ~70 s at 30 steps/s for that gap; --join-max-pause, default 60 s, refuses
  longer ones), or the session runs at 20 Hz during the join (section 7).

Labels: [F] read from the pinned Windows X4 9.00 executable (SHA-256 19750a65...6891) in this study, every address
pinned in lockstep_join.h and checked by test_join_native_image; [F-prior] from this repository's earlier modules and
reports, cited; [M] measured offline (synthetic); [I] inference. Decompiler output was read for analysis only and is
not in the repository.

1. WHY A LOADED WORLD IS NOT THE WORLD THAT SAVED
Catalogue of what save -> load changes, with the module that keeps it equal now:

1.1 LAYOUTS RE-DERIVED AFTER J1 BUILD 177 (read-only analysis of the pinned X4.exe; every offset below is checked on
the image by test_join_native_image [F]; offline: no X4 launched)
J1 build 177 (full defaults stack, pilot window after the save): the join request, SAVE_AT, the pins (against the file)
and X4's own save worked on both savers at T=633; then the side-table capture refused identically on both:
'tables:md:22:0/17904/0/0' (part md, site 22 = the cue key of the first heap entry; so far 0 contexts, 17,904 tree
nodes, 0 vector entries, 0 heap entries). Findings:
- Contexts (0 captured): the director's +0x148 is not the context list. It is a short vector of contexts flagged by
  AIDirector_Import 0x1069000 and the script refresh 0x10688b0 (AIDirector_RemoveContext 0x106f710 drops a finished
  one), normally empty. Every AIScriptContext is on the intrusive list AI::g_AIScriptContextList: head 0x6cf2808 (the
  first context), tail 0x6cf2de0, next ctx+0xb8, prev ctx+0xb0; the constructor 0x10631b0 appends, the destructor
  0x1063410 unlinks. The capture and the joiner's admission now walk that list (every link checked both ways, the
  tail compared); an entry that is no live AIScriptContext refuses (site 2), as a tree node's context always did.
  [I] A context that ended but is still referenced (its script reference count, ctx+0xa8) stays on the list until it
  is destroyed; whether the save keeps such contexts shows as ctx_missing/ctx_extra at the J1 joiner's admission.
- Wake vectors (0 and 0): the offsets hold (+0x200/+0x218 {begin, end, capacity}, index +0x230: RequestWake
  0x106f910, ClearWake 0x10669c0, Move). Move toggles the index each pass and drains the other vector until a pass
  finds its vector and the due timed wakes empty, so after Move both vectors are empty; at the save boundary only
  wakes requested later in the frame can wait there. 0/0 is the expected value, not a misread.
- MD heap entry: as assumed, {double time +0, u32 seq +8 (counter 0x38f52a4), u32 kind +0xc (1 condition check,
  2 actions, 3 pending state, 4 force, 5 signalled), Cue* +0x10 (0: cancelled)} (MDScheduler_Schedule 0x1122ae0,
  MDScheduler_Update 0x1121f80). The cue key was wrong. MD::Cue (vtable 0x2c75170): +0x150 its MD script (a static
  cue; 0 in an instance), +0x158 its parent (a static cue's static parent; a nested instance's parent instance),
  +0x160 its static cue (a static cue: itself), +0x168 a root instance's creator (the instantiating cue whose
  conditions or force made it), +0x170 its root instance (a root: itself; a static cue: 0), +0x180 state, +0x190 its
  sub-cues by static slot (an instance's mirror its static cue's: FUN_111dbb0), +0x1a8 the root instances it created
  (Cue_CheckConditions 0x111c290 and Cue_Force append; Cue_Destroy 0x1113900 moves the last entry into a removed one's
  place; the save 0x111a260 writes the list in order, the import 0x11121d0 rebuilds it in that order and sets each
  root's creator), +0x1c0 a static cue's every live instance (creation or load order: not portable), +0x1f8 the
  activation time. The old key looked every instance up in its static cue's +0x1a8: a nested instance (a sub-cue of
  an instance, most heap entries) is in no such list, and a root made by a nested instantiating cue is in its
  creator's list, not its static cue's; the first heap entry refused.
- The cue key now (join_cue_key, the same on savers and joiner; section 2): a root by its creator's key and its index
  among the creator's non-empty created entries, a nested instance by its root and its sub-cue slot path.
- Every refusal names its record (JOIN_RESULT detail on a saver, JOIN_STATUS rx_detail on a joiner):
  'tables:<part>:<site>:<ctx>/<wake>/<vec>/<md>,<record>,check=<check>,<raw fields>', e.g.
  'tables:md:22:6/0/0/2,md#2,kind=2,seq=39,time=9.5,cue=<p>,check=creator_index,root=<p>,creator=<p>,created=2,why=-1'
  (why -1 absent, -2 unreadable). Checks: list_head, list_prev, list_next, list_length, list_tail, ctx_null,
  ctx_vtable, ctx_id, tree_header, tree_right, tree_left, tree_parent, tree_up, tree_depth, tree_count, node,
  ctx_fields, vec_index, vector, entry, mask, md_heap, cue_vtable, def, state, root, parent, slot, creator,
  creator_index, creator_def, creator_depth, the static cue's (1.2: static_script, static_parent, static_depth,
  static_string, static_root, static_slot), memory. The joiner's:
  'contexts:<...>' and 'cue_key:md#<i>,...'. Pointer values go to the logs only, never into the package.
- Pins added (file hash and live check): the AIScriptContext constructor and destructor, Cue_CheckConditions,
  Cue_Destroy, the sub-cue instances 0x111dbb0, the MD cue import and export.
- Superseded by 1.2 (j1-7): a static cue is no longer keyed by the script's name map (its checks name, static_name
  and static_unnamed are gone).

1.2 THE STATIC CUE KEY AFTER J1 j1-7 (read-only analysis of the pinned X4.exe and of the game's MD; offline: no X4
launched)
J1 j1-7 (build 178 = smoke with 0005baca, the full defaults stack): the join save worked and the capture got far
(contexts 38,806, tree 17,904, vectors 0/0 as expected at the boundary, MD heap entries 0..178 keyed); both savers
refused at heap entry 179, identically in structure:
  'tables:md:22:38806/17904/0/173,md#179,kind=2,seq=11541,time=30,cue=<p>,check=static_unnamed,static=<p>,parent=0,depth=4'
(173 = the entries keyed so far: 179 less the cancelled ones). A static cue whose name lookup failed, and so did the
lookup of each of its three ancestors up to a root (parent 0). Findings:
- Every static cue has a name, in its own field. The game's MD (base and the six DLCs, resolved) has no <cue> without
  a name; the loader refuses one ('Unnamed cue in MD script %s, line %d', 0x1110170) and a duplicate in the same
  script (0x1110170 from XML, 0x11103d0 from a save). The name is the std::string at cue+0xc8 [F]: the XML constructor
  0x1111170 copies the name attribute (0x1111203), the MD import 0x11121d0 the saved one (0x111227a), a library user's
  sub-cue copy 0x1111dd0 its library cue's (0x1111e63/0x1111e6a; 0x11179e0 makes one copy per sub-cue of the library,
  parent = the user, +0x100 = the library cue), and the import's second pass 0x1118d70 gives a copy the save wrote
  without a name its library cue's (0x1118e9c, 0x1118f1d). Every static cue of a script has the script at +0x150 (the
  XML constructor 0x1111317, the import 0x111233e, a copy its parent's: 0x1111ef6/0x1111efd), whose name is the
  std::string at +0x18 (the script constructors 0x110dff0 from XML and 0x110e180 from a save); parents at +0x158
  (0x1111326, 0x1111f04, 0x111234d); a root has parent 0 (also a <library> nested in a cue's <cues>: built with parent
  0, never in a sub-cue slot, 0x11119ba). Siblings have distinct names, roots too (unique per script).
- 'static_unnamed' was the lookup, not the cue. The join (like script_rng_md) found a cue's name through
  npc_cue_name_with (lockstep_cue_name.h), which walks the script's name map (+0x60; insert 0x1110520 from all four
  constructors above; a library copy is entered as "<user>.<sub>") in key order (std::string compare 0xe8ff0) with a
  budget of 8,192 steps. An in-order walk costs about 3 steps per entry, so only the first ~2,700 keys in byte order
  are ever found. Map sizes counted offline from the game's MD (named cues and libraries plus every library copy; the
  live maps were not read) [I]: Story_Yaki 4,388, Story_Paranid 4,202, Story_Terraforming 3,498, Story_Criminal
  3,442, Story_Split 3,177, Story_Thefan 2,872 (X4Ep1_War_Subscriptions 2,576, FactionLogic 1,497, the rest less).
  In those six scripts the root 'Start' ranks 2,841..4,180 and 'Setup' (where there is one) 2,809..4,161, so their
  trees read as unnamed from the root down: each script has 25..154 chains Start/Setup.../<x>/<leaf> four levels deep
  with every name past the budget, 4..15 of whose leaves have a <delay> (heap kind 2: a static cue pending its delayed actions), e.g.
  Story_Criminal Start/Setup/Setup_Character_Maestro/PATCH_AddStoryMentors_Maestro. That is j1-7's record [I: the
  live record names pointers, not the script].
- The key now (join_static_key, lockstep_join.h): a static cue's key is FNV-1a 64 of the text
  "<script>.<root>.<...>.<cue>": the script's name and the own name (+0xc8) of every static cue on the chain from the
  root down to it (a nameless cue below the root, which the loader does not make, by "#<slot>" in its parent's
  sub-cue slots +0x190). It is the 'name' of a static cue's record and of every instance's record (its static cue's
  key), and the key of a static creator. Every level is checked: an MD::Cue (vtable), a static cue (+0x160 itself) of
  the same script. No address, no runtime ID, no map: the saver reads the names its loader built, the joiner the
  names the save wrote and the import restored. Keys of the same path agree across scripts only with the same script
  name. Package format unchanged (the record's name field; native_gate_join.py treats it as opaque).
- Refusals (replacing static_name, static_unnamed and name), each with the record and the static cue (static=):
  static_script (no script, or a script without a name: script=, size=), static_parent (a level that is no static cue
  of that script: at=, depth=, vtable=, def=, script=, expected=), static_depth (more than 64 levels: a cycle),
  static_string (a name unreadable or longer than 511 bytes: at=, size=), static_root (a root without a name: root=,
  depth=), static_slot (a nameless cue below the root that its parent's slots do not hold: at=, parent=, slots=, why=).
- Pins added (file hash and live check): the XML static cue constructor 0x1111170, the library copy 0x1111dd0, the
  import's second pass 0x1118d70, the script constructors 0x110dff0 and 0x110e180 (no base relocations in any of them);
  the layout bytes above are checked on the image (test_join_native_image).
- Elsewhere, not changed here [I]: the same budget makes script_rng_md (lockstep_script_rng_md.h, md_resume_rng's
  'unnamed=') read these story cues as unnamed, i.e. unkeyed. Both clients hold the same maps, so they agree on which
  cues those are, but the cues are not keyed. A budget from the map's size (+0x88) or the +0xc8 path would key them;
  that changes those modules' draws and is for their owners.

1.3 THE JOINER'S ADMISSION AFTER J1 j1-8 (offline analysis of j1-8's files: the joiner's log, the two packages and the join
save j633.xml.gz; no X4 launched)
J1 j1-8 (build 180 = smoke a6c41bd2, the full defaults stack): the save side passed (9.0). The joiner (lane b5, report mode,
slot 1's j633 files) loaded and was admitted, then stopped in the admission's own frame:
  JOIN_STOP ... based=1 imported=524268 saved=519578 created=3733 unmapped=957 ctx_missing=0 ctx_extra=6 wake_erased=17904
  wake_applied=17904 md_applied=243 md_missing=11987 md_extra=11991 md_ambiguous=0 clock_fixed=1 clock_delta=-5.75e-13
  rid_skipped=22904; SNAPSHOT_BIND_STOP bound=0 prepared=0 enrolled=0 failures=1; STOP slot=p3 completed=633 reason=74
Findings:
- MD pairing: the component that differed is the activation (+0x1f8). The pairing compared (name, root, ordinal, kind,
  state, activation). X4's save writes a cue's time with six significant digits (j633: time="28.383", time="1.06667"; the
  game clock likewise: clock_delta -5.75e-13 is 28.183000000000575 loaded as 28.183), and the loader's activation comes from
  it. Of the package's 12,230 live heap entries exactly 243 have an activation that survives that rounding ('%g' of the
  value is the value): md_applied 243; md_missing 11,987 is exactly the rest; md_extra 11,991 = the joiner's 12,234 live
  entries less 243 (4 more entries than the session's: not explained by the files; the new rows name them). So name, root
  and ordinal (the static path, the creator lists, the sub-cue slots) agreed for every entry [I: from the exact count; the
  next run's md_diff and rows confirm]. Kind and state stay in the key: both sides compute them alike (state 1 waits for a
  condition check, kind 1; state 2 for its delayed actions, kind 2; 11,721 and 509 in j633's package).
  Fixed (lockstep_join.h join_admit_md): the activation is no part of the pairing; the admission writes the session's
  activation bits into every paired cue (md_activation_fixed); a loaded activation further from the session's than the
  save's rounding (more than 5.0001e-6 x |activation|; j633's worst is 2.6e-6) counts md_activation_far (strict refuses
  'md_activation_far'). Each unpaired session entry is classified by the first key part the joiner has no counterpart for
  (md_diff=name:root:ordinal:kind_state in JOIN_STATUS), and up to four entries per side are logged with every key part
  (JOIN_UNPAIRED, gate.join_unpaired): the session's from the package (name, root, ordinal, kind, state, activation, time,
  seq, and the static path when the joiner holds that static cue), the joiner's with the same fields plus the static path,
  its root's creator (the creator's static path, 'inst:' for an instance creator), the root's index among the creator's
  non-empty created entries and that list's length, the sub-cue slots from the root down, the cue and root pointers.
  Only cues with a heap entry get their activation back: every other cue's saved time stays rounded on the joiner (8).
- Fixture bind (stop 74): not the IDs. X4 writes runtime IDs into a save (j633's fixture actors are 6c689..6c71d), but the
  joiner's import already records the session's portable key for each (join_import: the package maps 6c689 -> 176a13 ...
  6c71d -> 176cef, all class saved, all twelve in j633, 6c6fd a wreck), so the fixture's import capture saw the snapshot's
  saved IDs. The bind refused because it requires tick 0 (stats.issued 0) and the admission had just set the tick base
  T=633. Behind it: the preparation requires tick 1, the enrollment comes from the MD events of RestoreActors that the probe
  raises at tick 1 only (the cue ran before T), and every client stops (74) unless all twelve are enrolled by tick 4.
  Fixed (lockstep_snapshot_fixture.h): the bind runs at the tick base (0, or a joiner's T: join_tick_base); a joiner's actor
  without a navigation controller (a wreck at T) is bound without an Agent; the joiner prepares at T+1, registering its live
  actors with the radar, parameter and steering modules WITHOUT the session-start writes (gravidar_register_with keep,
  params_keep, flight_register_with init 0: the session made those writes at its tick 1), and enrolls all twelve (a session
  past tick 4 had them all); the stop check counts from the tick base. The probe's 'fixture' state provider hands the joiner
  the twelve actors (gate.snapshot_actor runtime IDs) and the readiness, refused unless the session's fixture list is the
  joiner's (SNAPSHOT_JOIN_ACTOR rows). A bind or preparation refusal names its stage and actor in SNAPSHOT_BOUNDARY
  (stage=bind_guard, bind_actor, bind_joiner_controller, joiner_identity, joiner_steering, ...). [I] That a wreck keeps no
  controller is an inference: j633's actor 8 (6c6fd) is one; should it keep a controller, the joiner refuses loudly at
  bind_joiner_controller actor=8 and the wreck needs its own form.
- Fail loudly on an ID that cannot be mapped: gate.join_load refuses fixture_unmapped:<id> (a fixture ID that is no saved
  identity of the package), fixture_early:<T> (T below 4) and boot_bind:world_trace|origin|transition (binds only the
  snapshot's tick 0 can satisfy). native_gate_join_overlays.py joiner checks every 16-hex-digit saved ID of the effective
  config (the session defaults applied, as the probe runs them) against the package's saved identities and refuses naming
  each key and ID (the overlay is renamed OUT.refused). j1-8's joiner config: 19 IDs (fixture 12; identity plan 4: three
  ships and the start sector 0x729; pilot slots 3), every one a saved identity of j633's package. The config keeps the
  snapshot's IDs (not rewritten to j633's): every client keys by them.
- ctx_extra=6 (ctx_missing 0): the join save holds exactly the session's 38,806 contexts (j633's '<script id= name=>'
  instances, per script name the session's CTX count), so the joiner's 6 extra contexts are in no save: started by its
  load or its loading frames [I: scripts of game-loaded MD cues or start-up scripts that ran on the savers' own snapshot load
  and ended before T]. A gap if they act (they run on the joiner only); strict refuses them. JOIN_UNPAIRED ctx rows now
  name their scripts, owner keys and entities.
- unmapped=957: imports whose save ID has no portable key in the package: 818 connection objects (IDs 0x15b903..0x165128,
  made during the session: NPC room positions, chairs, docking, zone visibility, control positions), 55 bullets in flight
  and 84 render effect objects (the local ID list, 0x1e0....). The session's table has no key for any of them either (made
  after the snapshot, outside the created table), so both sides key them NONE: fine for the keys; their runtime IDs differ
  on the joiner (8, the loaded objects' runtime IDs). The other way: 2,351 saved and 5 created identities of the package are
  not in the save (objects destroyed before T keep their table entries; never imported, no effect).

1.4 THE FIXTURE BIND AFTER J1 j1-9 (offline analysis of j1-9's files: saver 1's log, the joiner's log, slot 1's j633 package
and join save; no X4 launched)
J1 j1-9 (build 182 = smoke 3df97667): the save side passed again and the joiner's admission was nearly clean (imported=524462
unmapped=939 ctx_missing=0 ctx_extra=6 wake 17908/17908 missing 0, md_applied=12255 md_missing=0 md_extra=4
md_activation_fixed=12015 md_activation_far=0 clock_fixed=1 rid_skipped=22949), then it stopped at T=633 in the fixture
bind: SNAPSHOT_BIND_STOP bound=0 prepared=0 enrolled=0 failures=1; SNAPSHOT_BOUNDARY stage=bind_joiner_controller actor=8;
STOP completed=633 reason=74.
Findings:
- Actor 8 in the session (saver 1's log): saved 176c77, the session's runtime 6c6fd (SNAPSHOT_ACTOR id=8: bound at 0,
  prepared at 1, enrolled with the other eleven). It fought 6c69b (actor 2; INTERRUPT rows at 495..509). Its last ACTOR row
  is at 480 (hull 1688, trace stride 60); from 540 on its declared row is '8/dead/body0:...' (the probe's
  IsComponentOperational is false), with velocities at 540 and 600 and zero velocities from 660 on. The join save holds it as
  <component class="ship_s" ... state="wreck" attacker="[0x6c69b]" attacktime="23.983"> with <movement
  class="lineardeceleration"><deceleration time="5"/> ... <time start="24.016"/>: it died at game time 24.0163 (tick 508;
  tick 0 is 7.083 at 30 Hz) and its drift ends at 29.016, tick ~658, 25 ticks after T. What the session's gate holds for it
  at T: actor_components/actor_ids the wreck object and 6c6fd (bound at 0, never cleared); agent_samples and flight_samples
  the Agent of the NavigationController it had until its death (stale since: the death listener replaced object+0x200 with a
  U::LinearDecelerationController, lockstep_wreck_watch.h; the hooks match an Agent only through agent_identity); its radar
  unit and parameters as registered at tick 1; snapshot_dead unset (only a joiner sets it). The gate kept no dead/alive
  state of its own; the probe's classification enters the digest.
- The joiner after loading: the save carries the wreck's controller (23 of j633's 25 wreck ships have <movement
  class="lineardeceleration">, also those whose drift ended long before T, e.g. 0x1487a from 4.817; the other two, 0x72713 and
  0x772a0, were killed by attackmethod="lowattentionattack" and have no movement: a wreck without a controller exists too), so
  X4's loader gives actor 8's wreck a controller and object+0x200 is not null; the rule 'a wreck has no controller' (1.3's [I]) was wrong. [I] It is the
  LDC (vtable 0x2c3f5f0): the save's movement class, the Windows census of a loaded world (LinearDeceleration 404 controllers,
  LOCKSTEP-LINUX-WORLD-HASH-20261029.txt) and lockstep_path_transforms.h (176ad5's wreck: +0x200 an LDC). Then the check that
  failed is the controller's class (0x2c3f5f0, not NavigationController 0x2c51158). j1-9's log names no check; the
  diagnostic below names it in the next run.
- Fixed (lockstep_snapshot_fixture.h, lockstep_join.h): each fixture actor has a VIEW, what the gate binds: nav (a
  NavigationController passing every check: bound with its Agent), drift (an LDC), bare (no controller), other (another class,
  or a NavigationController failing a check), lost (the object is no longer found as the fixture's ship). The session's capture
  at T writes its twelve views into the package (GATE fixture[12], from the record's pad; in the portable digest as
  'FIXTURE|<12 digits>' when a fixture is bound, so the savers must agree; packages without a fixture keep their digest). A
  joiner's join_load refuses a package without views (fixture_view_missing: a saver build before them, e.g. j1-9's packages)
  or with a view it cannot bind (fixture_view:<i>:other|lost|unset). Its bind classifies its loaded twelve the same way and
  binds only when every view equals the session's: nav with the Agent, drift and bare without one (snapshot_dead; checked by
  object only at T+1, not registered with radar, parameters or steering: the session's Agent of the wreck updates no more).
  Any difference stops at stage bind_joiner_view (74). SNAPSHOT_BOUNDARY now ends with views=<session>/<bound> and the check
  that refused with its values, e.g. 'session=nav joiner=drift check=vtable ctrl=set ctrl_vt=2c3f5f0 owner=- slots=-
  parameters=- agent_owner=-' (check names: object:<map|imports|find|identity|class|macro>, controller_read, controller_null,
  vtable_read, vtable, owner, slots, parameters, agent_owner; ok when the navigation checks passed). JOIN_STATUS ends with
  fixture=<a saver's captured views> rx_fixture=<a joiner's package views> ('-' for none). native_gate_join.py reads and
  writes the views (gate 'fixture', twelve digits; summary), native_gate_join_overlays.py joiner refuses as join_load does and
  prints fixture_views=.
- Expected next, not visible in j1-9's files: the drift is saved rounded (start time at three decimals, 24.016 for 24.0163;
  the start pose at three decimals; velocities at six significant digits), so the joiner's wreck drifts on a slightly other
  path until ~T+25 and rests up to about 1.5 cm (46.6 m/s x 0.33 ms) from the session's: actor 8's '8/dead/body0' bits can
  differ from T+1 (the replay's digest check and the census name it). The fix would carry each fixture wreck's LDC fields
  (lockstep_wreck_watch.h WRECK_STATE: start transform, start time, velocities, duration, flag; 0x71 bytes) in the package
  and write them into the loaded LDC, as the MD activations are restored (1.3); not built.

1.5 THE PROBE'S STATE ON THE JOINER AFTER J1 j1-10 (offline analysis of j1-10's files: the joiner's x4lan-debug.txt, saver
1's x4lan-debug.txt at ticks 420-660, join/replay.log, the joiner overlay's config.lua.append; no X4 launched)
J1 j1-10 (build 183): the save side passed; the joiner was admitted with j1-9's counters (md_applied 12,255 md_extra 4,
ctx_extra 6, wakes 17,908/17,908), bound, prepared and enrolled all twelve (SNAPSHOT_JOIN_ACTOR x12, actor 8 'flight=disabled':
the wreck bound without an Agent, 1.4) and sent its first report (JOIN_REPLAY FIRST tick=633); in frame T+1 = 634 the probe
raised 'native_gate_probe.lua:2207: native health sample missing' (PILOT_STOP_WHY reason=91, replay joiner_command_exited_5).
Findings:
- Not the wreck: the actor that failed is actor 1, the first one sampled (the last rows before the error are WORLD_ACTOR id=1
  and its three WEAPON_REGISTER rows; there is no WORLD_ACTOR id=2), and X4 names the cause in the same frame:
  'GetComponentData(): Invalid argument #1 <component> (got cdata, expected component ID)'. The session's probe keeps each
  actor as ConvertIDTo64Bit of the MD event's object, which GetComponentData accepts; the 'fixture' provider of 1.3 built the
  joiner's as ffi.new('UniverseID', ...), an FFI uint64: the FFI calls took it, GetComponentData refused it, hull and
  shield were nil. The old probe in the new offline harness stops with the same line (2207) after the same refusal.
- Actor 8 in the session: live rows up to 480 (hull 67 %, shield 0), '8/dead/body0:...:/body1:...' from 540 on: after its death
  (tick 508) IsComponentOperational is false and, with strict physics, the dead row is the two physics bodies that gate.body
  reads natively (no GetComponentData, no hull or shield); velocities at 540 and 600, zero from 660. The joiner's row for it
  is the same function of IsComponentOperational and gate.body; j1-10 stopped at actor 1, before actor 8.
- The audit: what the probe and its adapters keep across ticks, whether it reaches the declared digest, and what a joiner
  does with it at T+1 ('-': nothing needed).

- Fixed (tools/dev/native_gate_probe.lua):
  'fixture' provider v2 'v2|<the twelve saved IDs>|<12 digits, 1 = the row at T was /dead>|<per actor the gun slots its
  sample registered, '.'-joined, '-' none; '/'-joined>'. The joiner takes each actor as ConvertIDTo64Bit(ConvertStringToLuaID(
  <runtime decimal>)), the value kind the session's event gave, refused unless it converts back to that runtime ID (below
  2^53); a session wreck must be no operational component here ('join: fixture actor <i> was a wreck in the session at T (its
  rows /dead) and is an operational component on this joiner'); the session's guns are registered with the gate at the
  restore and the sample registers no other: a gun of an actor live at T that is missing or that the gate refuses refuses
  the join; a wreck's gun that is gone moves nowhere, one the gate refuses (the session's registration of it is stale since
  the death) is logged. Rows: 'SNAPSHOT_JOIN_ACTOR <gate row> session=dead|live joiner=dead|live', 'JOIN_FIXTURE_GUNS id=<i>
  session=<slots> registered=<slots> absent=<slots> refused=<slots>'. A v1 fixture state (a saver before this build) refuses
  ('v2|ids|dead|guns').
  'world_hash' provider: the session's world_hash_state at T ('-' without a capture refuses), the world watches armed.
  'pilot_window' provider: 'v1|<start>,<stop>'; the joiner's window must be the session's and T+1 <= start-5.
  native_gate_join refuses native_gate_hybrid and native_gate_player_orders (Lua state without a provider), a joiner refuses
  native_gate_world_trace_ticks. j1-10's package has no world_hash line either: past the type fix its joiner would have
  stopped at T+1 on 'world hash baseline missing' (the session's last capture was at 600, the joiner had none).
  tools/dev/native_gate_join_overlays.py joiner refuses such a package before the launch, as the probe would in frame T+1
  (check_lua_state: lua_state:fixture:v1|, lua_state:world_hash:missing, lua_state:pilot_window:<line>|late; j1-10's
  j633.x4join: 'lua_state:fixture:v1|') and prints lua_state=<providers>.
- Known, not built (each shows as a digest difference the replay names, never silently):
  1. The guns' first step. The session registered its guns at tick 2; from tick 3 on every fixture gun Move ran with the
     gate's keyed RNG. The joiner registers them in frame T+1, after step T+1: the probe's frame runs in slot 6, after slot
     3's Moves, and the gate goes from the admission to step T+1 within one FrameTick, with no Lua frame between. So step T+1's
     fixture gun Moves take the all-ship actor RNG path on the joiner. j1-10's 830 WEAPON rows (every 60 ticks) show no
     fixture gun Move drawing (rng_before = rng_after): [I] it differs only for a gun that draws in step T+1. Fix: the gate
     registers the package's guns in the joiner's preparation (lockstep_snapshot_fixture.h, before the pass of T+1).
  2. The joiner's first own world capture (the first stride tick after T: 900 for T=633) differs by construction. The world
     hash's item list is this client's saved identity table plus every created object present at any earlier capture,
     cumulative (lockstep_world_hash.h world_hash_init, lockstep_world_created.h created_capture_rows). The session's at T
     holds the saved IDs of objects destroyed before T as absent rows (j1-8's j633: 2,351 of the package's saved identities
     are not in the save; j1-10's saver captured 522,294 objects at tick 1 = 521,929 saved + 365 created, its joiner imported
     519,578 saved) and the created objects its captures at 1, 300 and 600 saw that died since; the joiner's holds neither. Fix: carry the
     session's item set (its saved part is the package's IDS saved class; the created captured set is not in the package)
     and seed the joiner's list with absent rows (an absent row hashes type, flags and parent 0, whatever its runtime ID).
  3. Saved values (2a, 1.4): actor 8's drift; [I] also the fixture shields (the raw shield state: value, change time, rates),
     which the save writes with six significant digits, so a regenerating shield's bits can differ from T+1.

1.6 WHAT SAVE+LOAD CHANGES: J1 j1-11's CENSUS CLASSIFIED (offline analysis of j1-11's files: saver 1's and the joiner's
captures at ticks 1, 600, 900 and 900, 1200, the snapshot save_009 and the join save j633, slot 1's package; no X4 launched)
J1 j1-11 (build184 + the smoke tooling incl. 066d5a64) ran end to end: the save side passed (both savers at T=633, packages
equal), the joiner loaded j633, was admitted and replayed the session's journal 633..1799 at 24.05 steps/s (observe mode;
replay.json diverged, first_mismatch=634: the declared digest differs from the first tick after T). census_compare of the
joiner against saver 1 at tick 900: common 524,219, only left 2,562, only right 268, equal 328,494, differing 195,725 (motion
117,656, health 87,009, timing 6,840, topology 417).
Method. Tick 900 is 267 steps after T, so a difference there can be dynamics. For every differing object the session's
captures at 1, 600, 900 and the joiner's at 900, 1200 separate a value the load set and nothing touched since (static on
both sides, never equal) from one that moves. What each domain hashes (lockstep_world_hash.h): health = the hull (+0x280,
double) and the shield group the object reads (+0x290: value, changed time, maximum, delay, rate, factor, generator count,
modifiers; the canonical form of lockstep_state.h), then type, flags, parent; motion = the controller's vtable and its state
(a pure transform: four 16-byte vectors, getter 0x10ba20 at controller+0x10 or 0x46ff70 at +0x20; navigation: two banks;
none: 0); timing = attention (+0x1ec) and a nav object's deadlines. Where the preimage has few unknowns it was solved
against the logged SHA-256 by brute force ([F], exact); the two saves show what X4 writes ([F]); the rest is [I].

- No large class is a census artifact. The changed time of an empty group (cargo bays, build storages, stations) and of a full
  shield is inert today (no recharge reads it until a generator is added or damage rewrites value and time together), but
  it is a real field the session and the joiner disagree on, cheap to carry: restored exactly, not normalised.
- Weapon heat and ammunition are in no census domain (j633 writes lastshottime and ammunition per gun); no evidence either
  way, nothing built.
- j1-11's joiner logs hold no JOIN_ADMITTED line and no JOIN_UNPAIRED rows (only JOIN_LOADED and JOIN_STOP): the probe writes
  them in its first frame T+1 = 634, which the status stride (60) held, and a held tick's lines are dropped (ticklog). So
  j1-11's admission counters are unknown. Fixed: a joiner's first frame is always a written tick (native_gate_probe.lua
  ticklog.open), with or without --join-census.
- The comparison point is now the join itself (native_gate_world_hash_join, tools only): every client of the session takes
  one more world capture at T+1 (the T of each SAVE_AT it applied, whether it saves or not: the capture enters every client's
  declared state alike) and so does the joiner (its tick base + 1, its first frame; tools/dev/native_gate_probe.lua
  join_client.capture_at; the join core records every applied SAVE_AT's tick). Both sides have then run the one step T+1 from
  the world at T: the session's after its save (the save's own side effects included), the joiner's after its load and
  admission. A capture at T itself is not possible on a joiner from Lua (the admission and step T+1 run in one FrameTick with
  no probe frame between, 1.5), and a saver's frame T runs before its save. T+1 is a written tick (ticklog), the stride
  captures are unchanged, native_world_evidence.py accepts the extra ticks (join_capture_ticks), check_gate_options.py checks
  the key (true, with the stride and native_gate_join), native_gate_defaults.lua lists it as a diagnostic key.
  Harness: native_gate_join_overlays.py savers ... --join-census and joiner ... --join-census (both; it needs a --set
  native_gate_world_hash_stride=N), then census_compare.py SESSION JOINER --tick T+1 --left-client 1 --right-client 3.
  With a world hash watch list (the same --set on both overlays) census_compare decodes the watched objects' fields
  (watched_details: hull, shield encoding, the pose vectors); 9.3 has the J1 command with 50 representatives of the classes
  above.
- The world side table (package version 2; src/native/engine/lockstep_join_world.h; 2): captured on every saver after the
  side table and identities, for every present object of the identity table, keyed by its portable ID:
    HEALTH  every destructible object (the census health domain): hull bits; with a shield group its value and changed
            time in the census's canonical form at T (a recharge complete at T reads value = max, changed = 0, flag 2), so
            every saver writes the same record whatever its UI read; maximum and generator count as shape checks.
    POSE    every positional object whose controller is a pure transform (getter 0x10ba20 or 0x46ff70): the x, y, z
            lanes of its four vectors, as the census hashes them, with the controller's vtable and form. Navigation, dock
            and unknown controllers and objects of an unknown class are in neither section.
  Both are sorted by portable ID (the reader refuses another order: world_order) and enter the portable digest as one line
  each (count and SHA-256 of the records): the savers must agree. At the admission, after AI, MD and the allocator, the
  joiner finds each record's object through its own identity table, checks its class, the group's shape (present, maximum,
  count) and the controller's vtable and getter, and writes what differs: the hull, the group's value and changed time, the
  pose lanes (the w lanes untouched). A group several records read (a ship and its guns) is read back after all writes.
  Counters JOIN_STATUS world_cap=<health>/<pose> (a saver) and rx_version=, health=applied:hull_fixed:shield_fixed:
  missing:shape:conflict, pose=applied:fixed:missing:shape (a joiner); up to two JOIN_UNPAIRED rows per kind ('health
  side=session id=... why=missing', 'why=shape group= max= count=', 'why=conflict value= changed=', 'pose side=joiner id=...
  why=shape classes= kind= form='). Strict refuses world:health_missing, world:health_shape, world:health_conflict,
  world:pose_missing, world:pose_shape and a version 1 package (package_v1); report counts and continues. Pins (file hash and
  live check): the two pose getters and the shield read 0x79ddb0; the getters' vector loads are checked on the image.
  Size [I]: ~330,000 HEALTH records (48 bytes) and up to ~400,000 POSE records (64 bytes) for the census world: about 40 MB
  more package (j1-11's was 14.9 MB) and a walk of the identity table at the boundary.
- Expected next, at T+1: the health differences above gone (the groups' changed times and the rounded recharge times
  restored), the static poses equal; what stays: the ships' navigation banks (the save's rounded velocities), attention
  levels and nav deadlines, the item list rows, and anything the joiner derives from the loaded rotation before the
  admission (a physics or collision pose built at load from the rounded rotation is not in the census: [I], the census at
  T+1 cannot show it). The watched objects' decoded fields name each remaining field. A group whose maximum differs on the
  joiner (a sum over its generators in the load's order, j633 writes the groups in another order than save_009 [I]) is not
  written: 'health ... why=shape max=<joiner>/<session>' rows and health=..:<shape> name it; its maximum would join HEALTH.

- The navigation semantics [F, read-only analysis]: RefreshFromPathingState 0xea2a20 refreshes a controller once the game
  time passed its refresh time (+0xc0) and the read selector g_PathingStateBufferIndex 0x38f4ee4 differs from its cursor
  (+0xc8): it consumes the pending entry (+0x50 + 16 x selector, FUN_ea35e0 may post events) and the bank (+0x30 + 0x1a0 x
  selector: ApproachingWaypointEvent from +0x150, ArrivedAtWaypointEvent from +0x159/+0x15a) and sets the cursor to the
  selector. WaitAndSwapBuffers 0xeac170, the selectors' only writer, flips both in every frame with a non-zero step. So the
  absolute index is a count of frames since the process started, and only the cursor relative to the read selector and the
  banks by role carry meaning: the census's canonical form (lockstep_world_nav.h) is the right restore key. NAV writes the
  session's relation as the joiner's own read or write index and each role's bank into the joiner's bank of that role; the
  selectors are not touched. A pathing pass in flight would race: the gate joins every pass (native_gate_serial_pathing), so
  at the save boundary and at the admission none is [I from the gate's join, lockstep_gate.c].
- The attention semantics [F]: SetAttention 0x3d5660 posts ChangedAttentionEvent (MD event_object_changed_attention, AI
  on_attentionchange: both exist, md_keywords) and calls the class's attention-changed handler (vfunc 701: children's
  SetAttention, bodies). The session handled those events long before T, its scripts' state is in the save; posting them again
  at the admission would run the handlers on the joiner only. The levels involved (2, 3, 5) are below 7, where the per-level
  flags of vfunc 688/689 (bodies, per-frame work) are off on both sides, so ATTN writes the level and the due directly. A
  level of 7 or more differing at T would need the handler's body work; ATTN counts every level it writes (attn= in
  JOIN_STATUS, two sample rows with both levels and the class), so such a case would show.
- Health: placed exactly at T (1.6) yet differing after one step in a Xenon area where the session's health changes every few
  ticks: the per-frame driver is not in the package [I: the out-of-sight combat's timing, e.g. the weapons' lastshottime, which
  the save writes rounded, and the attackers' state]. SHIELD carries the group's remaining census fields (delay, rate, factor,
  the modifiers' factors), so a parameter difference is excluded or named (shield= and sample rows); the watch list below
  decodes ten of these ships at T+1 on both sides.
- Tooling: census_compare.py read the clients' schema from the installed config, which does not hold
  native_gate_world_hash_schema (the defaults stack sets WORLD_IMPORTED_2 at run time), so every row's raw navigation tuple was
  dropped as an error ('enriched Nav fields in legacy capture'); the absolute selectors and cursors above come from a re-parse
  with the enriched schema. Now: census_compare.py --schema WORLD_IMPORTED_2.

- The pathing manager [F: read-only analysis; the layout pinned in lockstep_join.h]: MoveWorker::Run kicks the pathing pass at
  the start of every frame (0xef4602: +0x88 = 0, release semaphore +0x18; the gate's serial join waits for it and then stamps
  every batched Agent's controller, lockstep_path_determinism.h), before AIDirector::Move and before WaitAndSwapBuffers flips the
  banks: a stamped ship refreshes from the bank that is the write role after the flip, which the census reads as relation 1.
  The pathing thread's Run 0xeabf00 sets its TLS clock from TimerBase, the pass time +0x200 and dt +0x1f8 (its clock minus its
  previous wake's), runs RunPass 0xeae3c0 unless the skip flag +0x208 is set or |dt| < 1e-4, clears the flag and releases
  semaphore +0x10. RunPass first assigns the new-agent list (+0x170 count, +0x178 sentinel; CreateAgent 0xeac530 appends, the
  loader calls it for every ship) round robin to the batches (AssignAgentToBatch 0xeaf2b0: vectors +0x180/+0x198 of 0x20-byte
  batches {flag, count, sentinel, cursor}, indices +0x1b8/+0x1c0; a node is 0x18 bytes {prev, next, Agent}), moves agents
  whose kind changed to the other vector, then updates every batch from its cursor (UpdateBatch; native_gate_path_budget = 100:
  the first 100 full, the rest cheap, the cursor moves to the first cheap one). The constructor and the load reset 0xeac1f0
  (NotifyGameReset) set the skip flag. So the first frame after any load updates and stamps nothing: the session's PATH_
  DETERMINISM read count=0 at its tick 1 and 10,029 at tick 2; the joiner's count=0 at 634 and 10,012 at 660. That explains
  all of (1): before the flip of step T+1 the joiner's 8,906 were neither stamped nor queried (911 were, by other MoveWorker
  code [I]: they refreshed as the session's); after the flip their cursor equals the read selector and nothing refreshes them.
  It explains the motion at T+1 [I]: the session's pass wrote a new state for every batched ship in step T+1, the joiner's
  wrote none, so the read role at the capture differs wherever a ship's next state differs from the restored one. And it
  keeps the motion apart afterwards [F]: the batch layout (members in list order, batch sizes, cursors by saved ID: the walk=
  hash of PATH_DETERMINISM) is equal on the two savers at every tick and different on the joiner at every tick 660..1500 (its
  batches were filled at T+2 from its load order, with its own 7 extra ships); with the count budget the layout decides which
  ships get a full update each tick.
- The hazard regions [F]: lockstep_region_rng.h's DamageField passes when the TLS time exceeds its due (+0x10f8) and damages
  every object in its zones in proportion to now - last (+0x10f0). The joiner logged REGION_RNG_ROW for every field at 634, each
  with prior due 0x403c2ed916872b02 = 28.183 = T: the loader sets every field's due to the loading clock, so every field passed
  in the joiner's first step with dt = 1/30 (the damage (float)(1/30) exactly at 1 per second). The session's own tick 1 shows
  the same after its load (36 rows, prior = its loading clock 7.083).
- Weapons and shield generators have no health state of their own here: each watched weapon's encoding equals its ship's
  group (1.6). The 4 combat objects are dynamics of step T+1 that the package does not carry (bullets in flight are in the save
  without a session key, 1.7; the attackers' weapon state) [I].
Built: package version 4 (lockstep_join.h, lockstep_join_world.h; native_gate_join.py): two more sections, in the portable
digest (PATH|n|sha, REGION|n|sha), keyed by portable ID, no pointer, no runtime ID:
    PATH    32 B  the pathing manager at the boundary of T (the pass of T joined): per batch vector its number of batches and
                  round-robin index; per batch its flag byte, member count and the cursor's index in list order; every member
                  in list order from the sentinel by its Agent's owner's portable ID (flag 1: no portable ID); the new-agent list
                  in order. The reader checks the whole structure (path_shape). The joiner (boot phase 3, the MoveWorker and the
                  pathing thread idle) requires the same number of batches and flags and every batch empty (its load's state),
                  finds each session Agent's node on its own new-agent list by the owner's portable ID, moves it into the
                  session's batch at the session's place (pure ring relinking: the node stays the engine's own allocation),
                  sets each cursor and both round-robin indices, keeps the session's new agents on the list in the session's
                  order and parks its own extra Agents on a ring of the gate's (no pass assigns or updates them; strict refuses
                  them). Then it wakes the pathing thread once with the skip flag set (state 2 and no pass in flight required):
                  Run takes the restored game time of T as its clock and pass time, runs no pass and clears the flag, so the
                  pass of T+1 runs with dt = 1/hz over the session's layout and the stamping refreshes the session's ships.

                  kind= at=v/b/i', 'path side=joiner id= why=extra', 'path side=joiner why=shape check=...' (batches, batch,

    REGION  32 B  every U::Region's (vtable 0x2b2ad60) DamageFields (vtable 0x2c4b2f0) in its field vector +0x288: the region's

- The flight model [F: read-only analysis; pinned in lockstep_join.h]: the Agent update 0xe8f250 first copies the read bank into
  the write bank, then, on a full update, calls the flight control model at Agent +0xf10: vfunc14(write bank, dt, game time, cheap,
  ...). A model of type 3 (U::Path::LinearFlightControlModel, vtable 0x2c4f610; j633 holds 9,997 of them, 10 steering, 3 spline)
  is not stepped on a cheap update (0xe848c0), so a linear ship moves only on its full updates, every fifth tick with batches of
  ~500 and 100 full updates per tick. The linear step 0xeb27c0 takes dt = game time - its own last step time +0x88 (the frame's dt
  only while +0x88 is -1) and stores the time: a full update after five ticks integrates five frames. It flies a leg: a new leg
  (+0x94 set, or a SetState target with +0x6c clear or moved past a threshold) records the start +0x70 and start time +0x80, fits
  the leg spline (Math::CompositeSpline<5> at +0xe0, a vector of 0xa0-byte Bezier segments +0xf0 and of parameter pairs +0x108,
  0xeb1e60: whenever the target is at least sqrt(2) m away) and resets the progress +0x90; each step advances the progress by
  speed x dt / length. The save writes <flightcontrolmodel type="linear"/> and nothing of this; the loader constructs a new model
  (0xeb1a10: +0x80 = +0x88 = -1, +0x94 = 1, an empty spline). So every joiner ship's first full update stepped one frame from a
  new leg where the session's stepped five frames along its leg: (5 - 1) frames x velocity, the watched ships' 0.133 s [I: the
  integration's arithmetic; F: the set of differing ships and the offsets].

  linear step 0xeb27c0 turns yaw, pitch and roll toward its basis with 0xeb1db0(angle, target, rate, dt): XMM2 = Agent +0x378,

- Where they live [F]: the Agent holds three 0x1a0-byte flight parameter slots, +0x000 and +0x1a0 (read and write by the pathing
  selectors 0x38f4ee4 / 0x2f4b6f4) and its own copy +0x340, which the step and the update read. A slot is the ship's flight

  the ship and its engines: accelerations, speeds, the turn rates +0x38..+0x40) and the Agent's +0x160..+0x19b (refresh 0xea1e80:
  a vector, a runtime ID +0x170, the refresh time +0x178, +0x180..+0x18c, the mode bytes +0x190..+0x193, +0x194, the travel speed
  +0x198). The update's tail 0xe8f020 copies the read slot into the own copy (0xe84970, +0x000..+0x19b) when the read slot's
  refresh time is newer than the own copy's; +0x140..+0x15f of the own copy is scratch the update clears after each update.

  PATH_DETERMINISM: count and order equal at 634 and 660, the stamps (the frame's refresh times) apart from 660; at 720 the
  session holds 10,001 Agents, the joiner 10,002; from 720 the order differs, at 780..900 the counts are equal again.
  The pass updates 102 Agents of each batch in full per tick from its cursor (1.9), so a member of a batch of ~500 is stepped
  every ~5 ticks and a linear model integrates its leg over dt = now - +0x88. One member removed (a death) shifts by one the
  members the cursor reaches each tick, and the shift grows by one per wrap: within the ~54 wraps from 634 to 900 the pass
  boundaries cross the whole list, every moving member's step partition changes and it flies the same leg with other rounding
  (census motion). A death at another tick on the joiner than on the session (or on one side only) shows as most of its batch;
  a death at the same tick on both sides as nothing. The deaths come from the combat, which differs from T+1 on: the shots in
  flight at T (no save holds them: the hit at T+1 is the first, health differs in 45 zones by 900) and the fixture's dogfight
  (the 13 ships above). So the independent source is the combat; the batches amplify each death at another tick into ~250..370
  census objects. The exact tick of each death on either side is not in j1-25's data (no census between 634 and 900).
- The projectiles [F: the weapon's shot vfunc998 0x8185b0, the zone's create vfunc930 0x83b500, the bullet setup 0xd50f30,
  U::Bullet::Move 0xd4f090 and UpdateFlight 0xd4f240 (lockstep_hit_native.h)]: a U::Bullet is a full component (U::Positional,
  a UniverseID from the component factory) created by the zone, then set up with its shooter (+0x2a0, refcounted), one draw of
  the thread's RNG for its spread when the macro has one, a movement controller allocated for its kind (a 0xb0-byte linear
  controller from the shot's transform, velocity and time; a StaticMovementController or a BeamBulletMovementController attached
  to the weapon for a beam), a heat factor +0x2a8; the shot then posts WeaponFiredEvent and spawns the muzzle effect;
  UpdateFlight casts along its path every 0.1 s (+0x288) and OnHit 0xd50500 damages. Recreating them at the admission is not
  built, and not a field copy: the package has no list of them (a bullet is in neither identity table the capture walks: it has
  no saved ID, and the created identities exclude class 11, lockstep_world_created.h), its macro is a per-process pointer that
  needs a portable key, the zone's create takes runtime IDs (it must run before the admission sets the session's next ID, or
  every later ID shifts) and appends each bullet behind the zone's other children where the session's sit in creation order
  (UpdateFlight's order within a zone decides same-tick hits), and the setup must run without its RNG draw and without the
  event. Cost, measured in j1-25: every hot join loses the shots in flight at T; here the first effect is one hit at T+1 (211.4
  shield on 0x73f92), and with the dogfight they make the deaths that fall at other ticks: the five batches' 1,298 of the 2,509 at
  900 [I]. Next: enumerate them at T (the zones' children of class U::Bullet) into a section, so a run counts them and the savers
  agree on them, before the joiner creates any.

  ship       model     at T+1 differ [F: watch rows]               |v| at T   session T+1   joiner T+1    active behaviour [F: STEER]

  0x7a8dc    spline    pos, velocity, angular velocity (+ axes)    351.298    351.298       346.373 (x 0.98598)
  0x7a8fe    spline    pos, velocity, angular velocity             260.222    260.222       256.573 (x 0.98598)
  0x176a74   spline    axes, velocity, angular velocity (not pos)    1.000      1.000         0.000
  0x73f92 + 3 weapons  health: stored shield 961.1 (session) / 1172.5 (joiner): the hit at T+1 of a shot in flight at T (1.13)
- Why the ten steering ships differ: the steering state is not in the model [F]. All ten have +0xa8 = 1 at T (STEER), so the step 0xec7200 does not
  integrate itself (its +0xa8 = 0 path is 1.13's clamp of velocity to the wanted one): it asks 0xe95fb0 for the Agent's motion solver (Agent +0xf80),
  writes the wanted velocities into it (0xe5d380, per axis) and calls the solver's step 0x1197fe0 / core 0x1197970, which writes the pose, the
  velocity and the acceleration back. The solver is a 0x18f60-byte object (NATIVE-INDEPENDENT-PLAYER-CONTEXT-20261027.txt, lockstep_solver.h): JPM
  controller subobjects (PathControl, LinControlA, VelControl3 twice, AccControl3, RotControlA, a VelControl) with their own velocity, acceleration
  and profile state, and a fixed-step clock: +0 the substep 0.02 s, +8 the remainder (the constructor sets it to 0.02, so 2 substeps on a first step
  at 30 Hz; after that the pattern 2, 2, 1). No save holds it and the loader does not build it: 0xe95fb0 allocates it at the Agent's first step (the
  constructor 0xd47ac0). So at T+1 the session's solver continues its history and the joiner's starts from nothing.
  The table shows it [I]: the joiner's speed barely moves at T+1 (a ratio to T of 1.00012, 0.99911, 1.00094, 1.00024) where the session's follows
  the commanded acceleration (1.01696, 0.99839, 1.00552, 1.00957): a fresh solver at zero acceleration under its jerk limits. Neither the avoidance
  cache nor a behaviour field is needed to explain T+1, and the data cannot exclude them either: every record has 14 contacts at T, the joiner's cache
  is refreshed at T+1 (+0x88 = -1). Only a run with the solver carried can separate them; the T+1 rows below name what is left.
- Why the three spline ships differ [F: j633.xml.gz, the step 0xeb7230; I: the mechanism]: the save writes <flightcontrolmodel type="spline"/>, nothing
  else, so the joiner's model has no route (both deques +0x70, +0x98 empty). On the session the velocity at T+1 equals the velocity at T to the last
  bit (a constant speed along the route); on the joiner both scouts lose the same 1.402% and 0x176a74 stops: with an empty route the step sets its state
  +0x218 = 8 and the solver (the same Agent +0xf80, through 0xe95fb0 and 0x1197970) brakes. So the spline models need their route rebuilt as well as
  the solver: not closed by the solver alone.
- Order, from this evidence: (1) the solver: one section, closes up to 10 of the 17 at T+1, also needed under the spline ships. (2) The projectiles: the
  five batches' 1,298 objects of about 2,500 at 900 come from deaths in NPC fights in other sectors (1.13), which neither the solver nor the spline
  routes touch: the 13 ships are one tempzone of the fixture. (3) The spline routes: three ships, engine-allocated deques, little effect on anything else.

- SPLM, SPLME, SPLMSEG [F: j1-27, 1.14; j1-39]: the spline flight models (SplineFlightControlModel 0x2c4f480, 0x4c0 bytes) with their
  routes: the two deques at +0x70/+0x98 ({proxy, map, map size, offset, count}, one 0x50-byte entry per block, each owning a heap
  CompositeSpline<5> (vtable 0x2b6c6d0; segments +0x10, pairs +0x28)), the embedded splines at +0x158/+0x1a8 and the request count. SPLM
  holds the model's bytes without its pointers and per-process fields (join_splm_skip), SPLME each route entry with its spline, SPLMSEG the
  segments and pairs (SPLINE's record). The joiner rebuilds each empty deque as the engine's push does (a map of a power of two entries
  above the count, blocks and splines from the engine's allocator 0x974c0 with this process's vtables). j1-38's crash: the avoidance cache
  (+0xf0 vector, +0x108 time) holds pointers into the session's world; it is left to the joiner (adb7c046). The FLIGHT admission no longer
  writes spline records from version 12 (SPLM owns them).
- GRAVN [F: j1-37, j1-39]: the zone groups' neighbour lists of the Gravidar worker's sector list (+0x68b8: 32-byte sector entries, groups
  of 0x58 bytes: zone, near +0x08, wide +0x20, neighbours +0x38). j1-39: 1,483 groups compared, 105 rebuilt in the joiner's indices; the
  posts did not change, so the neighbours were not the cause.
- GRAVP [F: j1-37, j1-41; 0x10b34c0, 0x10caef0, 0x3dde10]: the radar's scan gathers each zone's view children and the entry builder takes
  each object's position from the component view cache (object+0xe0+view*0x80, the pose pointer +0x28 to a 0x40-byte transform;
  g_ComponentViewCacheIndex 0x38f4eb4). 0x3dde10 composes from the published cache of the MoveWorker; on the session, objects outside the
  attended sectors keep older published poses, while the joiner's load publishes fresh ones. So at T+1 the joiner's scan saw other
  positions and other contacts. GRAVP carries, for every radar owner and its ancestors below the sector, both views' poses relative to the
  current view; the joiner writes them into its own poses. The portable digest takes which objects and views hold a pose, not the bytes.
  j1-41: 18,126 poses written, 12,862 already equal, none missing; the units posting changes on the joiner only fell from 230 to 9.
- SOLVER addresses [F: j1-37, j1-39, j1-40, j1-41 packages]: ship 0x176bf8's solver held a vector {begin, end, capacity} at +0x81c8 with
  the session's heap addresses; the joiner pushed into it at 1281 and crashed (j1-37 and j1-39). ad48cc59 kept the joiner's own value for
  every qword that looked like a user address (8-aligned, 4 GiB .. 2^47). That test also caught int pairs such as (0, 0x7a) and lost the
  session's data: 17,473 qwords over 15 solvers in j1-40, and the two motion differences at T+1 came back (ships 0x176b36 and 0x176ad5,
  drifts of about 1e-5 in bank 0). Most of the address-like qwords are stale heap values in the solver's scratch space (two solvers share
  a 2,000-qword pattern; no vector triple repeats across solvers). From version 12 the session checks each such qword against its own
  committed memory (VirtualQuery, one cached region) and carries a mapped one as JOIN_SOLVER_HEAP (0x8000000000000007); the joiner keeps
  its own value only there (5c583f7c; older packages keep the old test).
- GRAVQ [F: j1-42 script rows, 0x10acd70, 0x998430, 0x10b12c0, 0x99ab20, 0x99abe0]: the 137 rows of 634 on the session only were
  fight.attack.object.* stack handlers (src 4) of the 'Station Attacked' handler on event_gravidar_has_scanned. Slot 7 posts that event
  through FUN_140998430 (a std::list push_back: 0x40-byte nodes, next, prev, then kind 3, the source's runtime ID, the event, the time)
  into the worker's private list +0x69b0; the publish 0x10b12c0 at the top of the next MoveWorker frame splices it into
  U::g_EventCommandQueue 0x39943a8 (0x99ab20), and U::EventSystem_FlushCommandQueue 0x99abe0 delivers it. So the pass of T leaves its
  posts in that list at the boundary. GRAVQ carries the list in order (the worker idle under its ready lock); the joiner appends each
  has_scanned post to its own list. j1-44: 195 posts, the joiner's list already holding 2,638 entries of its own (the passive units its

1.18 THE RADAR'S GATHER AT T+1 (3 November; static analysis of X4 9.00, j1-63's package and save; J1 j1-64 with the coordinator's agreement)
- The gather [F: 0x10b34c0, 0x85dbe0, 0x79a470, 0xa47c60, 0x3d7010, 0x10b2aa0, 0x10b4100]: per sector entry of the worker's list +0x68b8, for
  each zone group (0x58 bytes, the zone at +0) whose zone has no 0x20000 in its live flags +0x1e4, the scan walks the zone's published
  children (the component view cache block object+0xe0+view*0x80: cached parent +0, children vector +0x10/+0x18, view flags +0x3c)
  recursively through 0x85dbe0. The static filter pair 0x2f6a650 {filter 0x2f6a608, mode 1} descends only into children that can contain
  objects (ComponentFilterByChecker<CanContainObjects>, slot 0x1340, compared to +0x40 = 0); the checker 0x2f58b08
  (ComponentFilterByChecker<IsCachedDockedInternally>, +0x40 = 0) takes a child that is a ship (IsA 0x49, slot 0x11e8) and not docked
  internally (Ship slot 1005: the cached parent IsA 0x28 and its slot 0x13a0, a docking bay's macro flag +0x5f9). An object whose live
  flags hold 0x20000 is dropped: the connect U::Component::vfunc624 0x3d7010 sets that bit when the component has no parent and clears
  it with one (it also marks the old and new parent's flags +0x11c, +0x19c, +0x1e4 with 2 or 4, the views' and the live copy).
  0x10b2aa0 finds or makes the object's radar unit (a passive AI::GravidarUnit 0x68 bytes when there is none or its +0x5c is set,
  posting UpdateGravidarAccessEvent); its +0x40 (size), +0x44 (factor), +0x48, +0x50, +0x60, slot 3 (+0x5c == 0: alive), slot 4 (+0xc4),
  slot 5 (+0xc0: the scan radius) and +0xb0 (next due) go into the 0xc0-byte gather entry; 0x10b4100 pairs the entries of neighbouring
  groups by distance against radius*factor plus both sizes. ActiveGravidarUnit slot 7 0x10acd70 publishes a unit's sorted contacts and
  posts GravidarHasScannedEvent when it lost or gained one.

  is 0x107e7 (its save ID), the joiner's 0x10cad. lockstep_runtime_ids.h: saved objects sit at a per-process ID offset, and
  "anything seeded, hashed or ordered by +8 therefore parts on saved objects"; its set mode makes the session's clients agree
  because they load the same snapshot, while the joiner loads j633 in its own order. The likely cause of this pick (and of others
  like it) is that the joiner's saved objects carry other runtime IDs than the session's. Giving them the session's IDs would
  mean rekeying each loaded object in g_UniverseIDMap (0x6cf1500) at the import (its saved ID is the session's runtime ID), with
  the slots the joiner's boot already took in the way: an allocator change to design and review, not yet attempted.

  go through one conversion, 0x13eb20 (from the attribute writer 0x138380, the script values' 0xcc1a40 and 32 other calls;
  precision -1: six significant digits, from 1000 up the integer digits and three; p < 100: p + 1 significant digits below 1, the
  integer digits and p above), and the load reads them through the XML reader 0x12fd30's parser 0x14c3090 (scale *= 0.1, not
  correctly rounded; 89 calls). The join save is read by the joiner only: while the saver's join save runs on its thread, the
  conversion's 34 calls go through thunk slot 112 and write 17 significant digits (a rel32 CAS each; 0x3b5d3c and the script
  values' 0xcc1dbc, whose rel32 crosses a 64-byte line, by a plain store inside the gate's boundary); from join_load to the
  admission the parser's 89 calls go through slot 1008 (the two crossing ones native) and a string of 16 or more significant
  digits is read exactly (strtod's candidate and its neighbours, the one whose 17 digits are the string's), shorter ones by the

  through the constructor returning to 0x3d1dd1), 90,362 connections (the maker returning to 0x9972f3) and 754,758 MD cue
  handlers (0xcaab18, no saved ID); the join save has id="[0x..]" on its 430,856 components and 90,360 connections. From
  join_load to the admission: the call before the class switch (0x8e320f, the component's node) and a connection node's name
  read (0x8e573b) put the node's id attribute (0xccff0, key 0x1fe) into a pending ID of their thread (thunk slots 144, 160;
  176 drops it after the connection's get-or-make 0x3d87e0, as the ID import does); the next AssignNewID of that kind takes
  the slot through InsertAtSlot 0xcc500 if it is unused, of the ID's generation, below the session's next slot and on the
  shared list, otherwise the load list (refused). Every other allocation takes the load list, block 29 of the map (past the
  shared limit; its frees go back on it). Before the load the shared list gets its blocks up to the session's next slot
  (EnsureFreeSlots); at the admission the head skips to that slot over the run the load left, holes and all. The ID import
  counts each saved object's ID against the save's. JOIN_STATUS ids=stage,nodes/connections/no_id,placed/refused,equal/
  other,load allocations/free,appended,skipped. Pins: the import's three regions, the attribute lookups, the constructors'
  calls (the image test tampers each).

  j1-169 07a405aa (build-hj17k)   the same with PEND

- The weapons' gimbals [F: JOIN_WATCHATT a208=/a48= (the animation container +0x208 and its skeleton +0x48), JOIN_WATCHANIM (every

  every joint of every object with a skeleton (the walk checks each joint's vbtable); the admission writes them where the joiner's
  skeleton has the same group and index. Status ikv=written:equal:missing:shape.
  j1-174 local (build-hj17p)   every side has the weapons' skeleton (one group each)
  j1-175 local (build-hj17q)   the joints: the joiner's at rest (value 0, rate 0, the load time), only +0x08..+0x1f differ
  j1-176 db5fb120 (build-hj17r)  IKV: ikv=99:15:0:9; the shot at 650 equal; first mismatch 720 (651 before); 900 differing 51 (55)

- Why the joiner keeps the pods [F: j1-179 (join watch on the pod over T+1..T+64), JOIN_WATCHREFS (a scan of committed private
