2. WHAT THE PACKAGE HOLDS (lockstep_join.h; reader tools/dev/native_gate_join.py)
- File <save folder>/mpjoin/j<T>.x4join next to j<T>.xml.gz. Little endian: 96-byte header (magic X4MPJN01, version 9
  (8 before STEER and STEERPT, 7 before PARAM and DOCK, 6 before ROUTE, 5 before AGENT, 4 before FLIGHT and SPLINE, 3 before PATH and REGION, 2 before SHIELD..KEYS, 1 before the world side table:
  still read, refused by a strict joiner),
  section count, T, game and real bits, the save's SHA-256 and size, the saver's slot, flags: 1 = diagnostic (an unaudited or
  unsupported module was armed), then 20 sections (version 6: 19, version 5: 18, version 4: 16, version 3: 14, version 2: 10, version 1: 8) {kind, count, bytes, SHA-256,
  payload}, then the SHA-256 of everything before it. Version 8 has 22 sections, version 9 24.
  1 GATE     one 256-byte record: T, clocks, wire shape (protocol, N, k, tick rate), MD sequence counter, runtime-ID next,
             created table generation/size, counts, the declared digest of T, the portable digest, save and capture us,
             the session's view of each snapshot fixture actor at T (12 bytes: 1 nav, 2 drift, 3 bare, 4 other, 5 lost;
             all 0 without a bound fixture; 1.4)
  2 IDS      (runtime ID as written in the save, portable ID, class 0 saved / 1 created), sorted by runtime ID
  3 WAKE     tree order: owner key, script hash, due bits, script version, reason, flags (2: due != node key,
             4: context's node != node; diagnostics)
  4 WAKEVEC  per vector in order (holes dropped): owner key, script, version, vector, position, wake mask
  5 MDHEAP   array order (cancelled entries dropped, counted): cue key (static name hash, root key, ordinal), time,
             activation, seq, kind, state
  6 MODULES  one line per armed module: serialized, superseded, stateless, unsupported, unaudited
  7 CTX      the key of every context on AI::g_AIScriptContextList (0x6cf2808), sorted (the joiner's multiset must
             equal it)
  8 LUA      the probe's state providers (native_gate_join_core.lua X4LAN_JOIN_CORE.provide), one line each
  9 HEALTH   (version 2) every destructible object, sorted by portable ID: portable ID, hull bits, its shield group's value
             and changed time (the census's canonical form at T), maximum, flags (1 a group, 2 full form), generator count
  10 POSE    (version 2) every positional object with a pure-transform controller, sorted: portable ID, the controller's
             vtable RVA, form (1 vectors at +0x10, 2 at +0x20), the x, y, z lanes of its four vectors (lockstep_join_world.h)
  11 SHIELD  (version 3) every destructible object with a shield group, sorted: delay, rate, factor, the modifiers' factors
  12 NAV     (version 3) every navigation controller, sorted: refresh, cursor relation, parameter and probe dues and enables,
             both pathing banks by role (vectors, references and pending entry as portable IDs, count, mode, eta, flags, layer)
  13 ATTN    (version 3) every present object of a known class, sorted: attention level, an Object's attention due
  14 KEYS    (version 3) the gate's first-call tables, sorted by (table, key): attention_all, nav probe and parameter inits,
             the fixture actors' attention and navigation first refresh (1.7)
  15 PATH    (version 4) the pathing manager, sorted by (kind, vector, batch, position): per batch vector its batches and
             round-robin index, per batch its flag, member count and cursor index, every member by its Agent's owner's
             portable ID in list order, the new-agent list (1.8)
  16 REGION  (version 4) every hazard region's damage fields, sorted by (region, field): last and next pass time (1.8)
  17 FLIGHT  (version 5) every navigation Agent's flight control model by its owner's portable ID: kind, a linear model's target,
             leg, step clock and spline scalars, the spline's vector lengths (1.9)
  18 SPLINE  (version 5) the linear models' Bezier segments and parameter pairs, sorted by (portable, part, index) (1.9)
  19 AGENT   (version 6) one per FLIGHT record: the model's Agent's step inputs (speed, acceleration and turn limits, modes,
             the re-target flag, waypoint, pending, group and formation lengths) (1.10)
  20 ROUTE   (version 7) per AGENT record a head (the model's SetState word, the Agent's IDs +0xef8/+0xf08/+0xf48/+0xf60 as
             portable IDs, bytes around the waypoints) and the Agent's waypoint and pending waypoint entries, references as
             portable IDs, sorted by (portable, kind, list, index) (1.11)
  21 PARAM   (version 8) one per AGENT record: the Agent's three flight parameter slots by role (read, write, its own copy +0x340),
             +0x170 as a portable ID (1.12)
  22 DOCK    (version 8) every dock and undock controller, sorted by portable ID: kind, start time, animation, options, the x, y, z
             lanes of its vectors (1.12)
  23 STEER   (version 9) one per FLIGHT record of a steering model: its carried scalars and vector lanes, its target IDs as portable
             IDs, its two behaviours by class with their constructors' fields, the lengths of its path and avoidance cache (1.13)
  24 STEERPT (version 9) those models' path points as ROUTE's points (list 2), sorted by (portable, index) (1.13)
- Keys never contain a pointer or a runtime ID. Context key = (owner class, owner portable ID, script name FNV-1a,
  script version). Cue key = (name, root, ordinal) (corrected after J1 build 177, 1.1, and j1-7, 1.2): name = the
  static cue's path key, FNV-1a of "<script>.<root>.<...>.<cue>" (an instance: its static cue's). A static cue: root 0,
  ordinal 0 (the joiner pairs by name, root, ordinal, kind and state, not by the activation the save rounds: 1.3).
  An instance: root = the key of its root instance R (+0x170) = the key of R's creator C
  (+0x168; a static C by its path key, an instance C by its own cue key, up to 8 creators) mixed with R's index among the
  non-empty entries of C's created list (+0x1a8, as the save writes it); ordinal = 0 for R itself, else a hash of the
  sub-cue slots (+0x190) from R down to the instance through the parents (+0x158). A static cue has at most one
  instance in one root's tree, so the key names every cue. Repeated keys with different captured values are counted
  ambiguous (GATE ambiguous, status).
- Portable digest: SHA-256 of the client-independent lines (CTX, WAKE, WAKEVEC, MDHEAP in pop order without seq,
  FIXTURE|<12 view digits> when a fixture is bound, and in version 2 HEALTH|<n>|<SHA-256 of its records> and POSE|<n>|<...>,
  in version 3 also SHIELD, NAV, ATTN and KEYS lines, in version 4 PATH and REGION, in version 5 FLIGHT and SPLINE, in version 6 AGENT, in version 7 ROUTE, in version 8 PARAM and DOCK, in version 9 STEER and STEERPT), first 16 bytes. Every saver announces it;
  the relay refuses the join when two savers differ (native_gate_join.py compare names the differing world records by portable
  ID; PATH by kind, vector, batch and position, REGION by region and field).

3. THE SAVE AT THE BOUNDARY (Windows) AND THE PAUSE
- The relay places SAVE_AT(k, T, mask) in absent slot k's stand-in blob at the next tick P it fills; every client gets
  it with the permit P+1+lookahead; T = P+1+lookahead+lead (--join-lead, default 30). The client's probe calls
  gate.join_arm(T, k, mask, save folder, mode) on the UI thread (the MoveWorker; its TLS block is kept).
- At the gate's FrameTick boundary after step T (main thread, MoveWorker joined), before the report of T:
  pins checked (AIDirector::Move, SetTimedWake, ClearWake, RequestWake, tree insert/erase, MDScheduler_Update/Schedule,
  heap pop, Cue_CreateInstance, Cue_ResumeAfterLoad, GameClass::Save and its only call site, IsSavingPossible),
  no engine save pending (countdown 0x3d198a8 = 0), IsSavingPossible(game, 0) == 1, folder mpjoin created, the
  one-time 'gameModified' byte 0x31c20c7 cleared (status modified_cleared), then GameClass::Save 0x9f0dd0(game
  0x3d196f0, "mpjoin\j<T>", "MP join <T>", online 0) with the MoveWorker's TLS clock +0x300, view +0x314 and RNG
  word +0x318 copied onto this thread for the call and restored after it. Then the file's SHA-256, the capture
  (after the save, so the save's own side effects are in it), the package, its SHA-256. A failure of any step sets
  state=failed with a reason; the session never stops for it.
- The pause: everyone saves at T, so every client stands still for its own save; the others wait for the slowest
  (their relay timeout native gate timeout must exceed it; 60-120 s recommended for J1). Capture cost [I]: a walk of
  ~20,000 contexts, a tree of the same order, an MD heap of a few thousand entries and up to ~1 million identity
  rows (qsort): well under a second; the package is ~25 MB at a million identities.
- Probe state: in frame T (before the report) the probe hands gate.join_lua(T, X4LAN_JOIN_CORE.state_text()) to the
  gate; the identity adapter provides its setup/fold flags, plan digest, MD result digest and slot ship IDs.
- Linux (the VPS server): gate_fork_save.h writes the save from a forked child without stopping the session, but no
  side table or package yet: the join core's arm refuses on Linux (FAILED 'not_armed'), so a session whose saver set
  includes the Linux gate refuses the join loudly. Porting join_capture_* into the fork child (it can read the frozen
  world exactly like the Windows capture; the Linux addresses of the AI director, MD scheduler and counter are needed)
  is the next step for the server path (J3).

4. THE JOINER
- Boot: the lane tool fetches j<T>.xml.gz and j<T>.x4join from the saver named in NGJOINSTATE, checks the package
  SHA-256 (python tools/dev/native_gate_join.py verify PACKAGE --sha SHA --save SAVE), stages the save as the snapshot
  (native_gate_snapshot_sha = the save SHA-256 named in the package) and builds the overlay with
  build_native_gate_overlay.py --join-package PACKAGE --join-package-sha SHA [--join-mode strict|report] (same keys
  as the session, same N, k and tick rate; the package refuses another wire shape).
- Before the load request the probe calls gate.join_load (every hash, every record shape, the portable digest, the
  announced SHA-256; a diagnostic package refuses a strict joiner). During the load the import observer records, for
  each imported object, the session's portable key of the save's ID: a saved ID of the boot snapshot as before, a
  created ID into the created table, nothing for an object the session had no portable key for (counted).
- Admission (boot phase 3, main thread): clocks (restored when they differ by less than 1 ms, else refused),
  context key multisets compared, the tree emptied through the engine's erase and refilled through its insert in the
  captured order, the vectors rewritten in place (positions and wake masks), the MD heap entries paired by cue key and
  given their time, seq and activation (1.3), re-sorted (a sorted array is a valid heap), the counter set, the runtime-ID head skipped
  to the session's next ID (set mode), then stats.issued = completed = report_tick = T. Its first report is tick T
  with the boot digest; the relay checks its clocks there, and from T+1 its declared digest. The snapshot fixture binds
  right after the admission at T and is prepared and enrolled at T+1 (1.3); JOIN_UNPAIRED rows sample what did not pair.
- Until the handover tick its own proposals are not in the permits (the relay's stand-in is): the permit check
  skips its own slot for those sources (gate_proposal_any; the lookahead window also accepts sources it never
  sealed). The probe restores its Lua state providers before its first tick T+1.

5. THE RELAY (native_gate_relay.py --join; native_gate_join.py JoinBarrier, JoinCoordinator)
  --join --absent K[,K...] [--join-savers S,...] [--join-lead 30] [--join-handover-gap 60] [--join-handover-lead 8]
  [--join-max-pause 60] [--join-catchup-limit 300] [--join-joiner-timeout 900]; protocols 3 and 4 only (refused with
  --adaptive, --wire-crypto, --live-inputs, --peer-window).
- Absent slots: once every present slot reported tick t, each absent slot's row is the reference slot's game, real and
  digest with the blob the relay scheduled (a JOIN record) or ''. They are journaled like any row (--input-journal),
  so the journal is exactly what every client applied.
- Launcher: NGJOIN|1|token|k|nonce (16 hex) -> NGJOINSTATE|1|token|k|nonce|state|T|saver|bytes|package_sha|detail;
  resend to poll. One join at a time (busy), only absent slots (slot_not_absent).
- Save: SAVE_AT, then the savers' SAVED records (scanned in released rows); all savers must announce the same portable
  digest (saver_disagree); a FAILED record refuses with its reason; no record within 4 x lead ticks after T: timeout.
- Catch-up: the joiner's reports go to the coordinator until H: its first report must be tick T; each report is checked
  against the session's reference row (game and real bits exact; digest from T+1 on) and answered with the permit(s)
  the session itself received for those ticks (T+1..T+1+k for the first, t+1+k later); nothing paces it.
- Handover: when the joiner is within --join-handover-gap of the live tick (or after --join-catchup-limit seconds with
  an expected pause within --join-max-pause), HANDOVER(k, H) goes into slot k's next stand-in blob, H = P+1+k+
  handover lead. Rows of slot k before H stay stand-ins, from H on they are the joiner's: the live session waits at
  H for the joiner's report of H (the bounded final pause), then slot k is a client like any other.
- Failure after the announcement: an ABORT record, slot k becomes a stand-in again (its rows already reported stay),
  the session continues.

6. FAIL-LOUD CHECKS (in order)
  relay start      --join with an unsupported wire; savers not present
  SAVE_AT          arming refused (late tick, busy, protocol, Linux): FAILED not_armed
  saver boundary   strict + unaudited/unsupported module: FAILED unsupported_module; pins; engine save pending (busy);
                   IsSavingPossible (not_possible); engine save false (save_failed); file missing; capture; package write
  relay            savers' portable digests differ (saver_disagree); FAILED from any saver; timeout
  launcher/tool    package SHA-256 (announced), save SHA-256 (in the package), native_gate_join.py verify
  joiner load      package hash, every section hash, shapes, portable digest, wire shape, diagnostic in strict mode;
                   with the snapshot fixture: fixture_unmapped, fixture_early, fixture_view_missing, fixture_view:<i>:<view>
  joiner admission ids_foreign (fewer than half the imports mapped), created entries, clock beyond 1 ms, contexts
                   differ, wakes or vector entries without their context, MD heap entries unmatched or ambiguous,
                   runtime-ID head (strict; report mode counts and continues)
  joiner bind      a fixture actor whose loaded view is not the session's (bind_joiner_view, stop 74, both views and the
                   failing check in SNAPSHOT_BOUNDARY)
  catch-up         game, real or digest differ at any replayed tick: NGSTOP to the joiner, join failed, session on
  handover         expected pause above --join-max-pause: refused; joiner silent beyond --join-joiner-timeout: given up
  every stage      JOIN_STATUS / JOIN_STOP lines (gate.join_status) with every counter; NATIVE_GATE JOIN lines (relay)

7. CATCH-UP SPEED AND PAUSE (estimates; J1/J2 measure)
- Inputs [F-prior]: Linux census frame p50 23-47 ms unpaced; Windows ~28 ms per step with two instances on one PC
  (LOCKSTEP-NONBLOCKING-SAVE section 5); protocol 4 at k=1 reached 24.8 steps/s with latency (LOCKSTEP-ADAPTIVE-DELAY).
- Gap at the joiner's first report: G = (save s + copy s + load s) x hz. Windows lanes on one PC: save ~10 s [I],
  copy ~0, load ~60 s [I; the gate allows 600] -> G ~ 2,100 ticks at 30 Hz, 1,400 at 20 Hz.
- Joiner speed: unpaced, ~1000/28 ~ 35 steps/s [I] with three instances on the PC (a third X4 also slows the others).
- 30 Hz session: margin ~5 steps/s -> catch-up ~420 s without a pause, or the handover after --join-catchup-limit with
  an expected final pause of (G + lead)/35 - lead/30 ~ 60 s. 20 Hz session: margin ~15 -> ~95 s, no pause.
- Total wait of the session for one join: the save (all clients, ~10 s [I]) + the final pause (0-60 s); the joiner's
  own boot and catch-up run while the others play.
- Faster later: rendering off during catch-up (the discard layer gave the VPS ~23 fps against ~1), a lower session
  rate while a join runs, or the Linux server's fork-save (no save pause).

8. WHAT REMAINS (most important first)
1. Equality is unproven: J1 measures the loaded world against the running one. Known gaps with their counters:
   attention dues and nav deadlines from the loading frames (modules attention/nav marked unsupported), the Gravidar
   cursor (ai_schedule unsupported), event-driven cue listener order (md_queued in GATE), the loaded objects'
   runtime IDs, every module marked unaudited (MODULES lists them). Not in the package (1.13): the shots in flight at T (each
   death they cause at another tick shifts a whole pathing batch's census motion), the spline models' state, the obstacle
   publisher's lists behind the avoidance caches.
2. Cue keys (1.1): the key assumes the joiner's created lists and sub-cue slots are the session's at T (the import
   rebuilds the saved lists in order). A root created or destroyed between the joiner's load and its admission (the
   loading frames' MD update) shifts its creator's indices (Cue_Destroy moves the last entry into the hole): every cue
   of the shifted roots' trees shows as md_missing/md_extra at admission (strict refuses). Static cues are keyed by
   their script and name path (1.2), which the save carries; a script or cue renamed between the session's and the
   joiner's MD (another game or mod version) shows the same way. The activation is no longer part of the pairing (1.3).
2a. Rounded saved values: the save writes times with six significant digits. Restored for the heap's cues (activation),
   the clocks (clock_fixed), the shield groups and hulls (HEALTH) and the static components' rotations (POSE, 1.6) and
   everything else the side table carries; every other saved time (a completed cue's time, an object's attack/spawn times,
   a gun's lastshottime, ...) is the session's rounded on the joiner.
2d. A flying ship's navigation state: built (NAV, 1.7) for the census's fields by bank role, and the pathing manager's batches
   and the first pass after the load (PATH and one wake, 1.8), the linear flight model's leg, step clock and spline (FLIGHT,
   SPLINE, 1.9), the Agent's step inputs (AGENT, 1.10: the words written; from version 7 the flags too), the Agents' waypoint
   and pending waypoint entries, the IDs and bytes around them and the model's SetState word (ROUTE, 1.11). Not carried: the
   steering and spline models' state (13 ships in j633), a waypoint's connection pointer, the Agents' other state (event
   timers, avoidance), the formations' and groups' own state (slots), the banks' other bytes, the physics body's pose (built at
   load from the rounded state), a fixture wreck's drift (1.4).
2e. Attention: built (ATTN and KEYS, 1.7), levels written directly (no ChangedAttentionEvent); a differing level of 7 or more
   would also need the attention-changed handler's body work (counted, sampled).
2g. The joiner's own load-time creations (1.7: seven ships with parts and crew, their AI contexts, four MD patch cues): named
   and refused (created_extra, ctx_extra, md_extra), not undone; their pathing Agents are parked in report mode (PATH, 1.8).
2h. The Xenon area's health after one step (1.7): the hazard region's damage fields, whose pass times the loader resets
   (REGION, 1.8). Not carried: the combat of step T+1 (j1-14: one ship and three of its parts).
2f. Anything X4 derives at load from a rounded rotation (a collision or physics pose of a station module) keeps the
   joiner's value after POSE [I]; the census does not hash it.
2b. The fixture's tick-1 state: the twelve actors' radar unit times, navigation parameters and steering constructor draws
   (and lockstep_flight.h's per-actor init generation, which keys the next steering model's draw) are not in the package;
   the joiner keeps what its load gave them and does not repeat the session's tick-1 writes (1.3).
2c. Contexts the joiner's load starts (j1-8: ctx_extra 6, in no save) run on the joiner only.
3. The save's thread context: GameClass::Save on the main thread with three TLS words of the MoveWorker; other
   thread-identity uses inside the save would show as a save failure or a joiner difference.
4. Probe and adapter state (audited in 1.5): providers fixture (v2: the actors' value kind, the session's wrecks, its
   registered guns), world_hash, identity, pilot_absent and pilot_window carry it; the rest is per tick, log only or
   derived; hybrid sectors and the player orders adapter are refused with a join, the world trace on a joiner, a join past
   the pilot window's take-2 in Lua as well. Not carried yet: the guns' registration for step T+1 (the joiner registers
   them in frame T+1) and the world hash item list (the joiner's first own capture differs by construction): 1.5.
5. Absent slots in the per-slot modules: built (8.1). Not built: a plan that creates slot k's ship only at H (identity
   steps at H); an absent slot whose AI-held ship is lost before H stops at its seat (161 absent_ship_lost) instead of
   being enrolled dead; a respawn for it is the death module's only after its enrollment.
5a. A joiner inside a running pilot window: the records in flight at T (captured before T, applied after it), the parked
   controls, the slots' players and seats and the stock module's rings are not in the package, so a joiner whose T+1 is
   past take-2 (the window's start - 5) stops at once, loudly (91 join_inside_window). A join whose save tick lies before
   the window runs the window's start with everyone (9.1 J2: the window is moved after T). Carrying that pilot state in
   the package (a PILOT section: rings, seats, controls, the stock and UI replay queues) is the next step for joins
   into a running window.
6. Linux: no side table in the fork child, no Linux joiner (the gate's join module is Windows-only); the relay side is
   platform-independent.
7. Wire crypto, peer links and adaptive protocol 5 are refused with --join.
8. The catch-up margin (section 7).

8.1 CHANGES AFTER THE FIRST LIVE RUNS (1 November; offline: no X4 launched)
j1-2 (full defaults stack, 3 slots on two lanes, slot 3 absent): both clients stopped at tick 40, the window's first
applied record, with 155 stock_missing_slot; j1-3 (native_gate_pilot_stock=false) at 39 with 91 missing_record. Every
per-slot module required every slot's record every tick, and an absent slot's row is the relay's stand-in ('' or a JOIN
record). j1-4 (native_gate_pilot_inputs=false) saved nothing: JOIN_RESULT failed|633|3|...|5|pins on both savers.
Absent slots (lockstep_pilot_inputs.h; every pilot layer):
  source     native_gate_join.absent = {K,...} (build_native_gate_overlay.py --join-absent, written by
             native_gate_join_overlays.py on the savers' and the joiner's overlay from the relay's --absent; the driver's
             handoff carries 'absent'). The probe gives the mask to the gate when the pilot modules arm
             (gate.pilot_absent, before the first tick; PILOT_ABSENT_ARMED mask=), JOIN_ARMED logs absent=<mask>. Slot 1
             is never absent. The same list on every client and on the joiner.
  HANDOVER   the join section's HANDOVER(k, H), applied at tick a on every client and on the joiner in its catch-up,
             calls gate.pilot_handover(k, H): refused (the probe stops) unless k is absent and H >= a+3 (the relay's
             --join-handover-lead, default 8, now refused below 3). Slot k is then enrolled at H: its player is built at
             H-2 and seated at H-1 (as take-2/take-1 for the others; at take with the others when H <= take), its own
             client proposes from H (its first capture at H) and its records are required from H+delay.
  ABORT      gate.pilot_abort(k): its rows are stand-ins again from the row that carried the ABORT (a-1-lookahead), its
             records stop from there; the seat stays (neutral); an abort before H-2 undoes the enrollment.
  package    a state provider 'pilot_absent' (from, until, enroll per slot): a joiner takes over an earlier join's handover.
Until its handover an absent slot, on every client:
  pilot inputs (91)    no record needed or accepted (absent_record), its ship not driven (the identity plan's AI holds it);
                       its own client (the joiner) captures none ('absent')
  stock (155)          no record needed (receipt S<k>:0:0..0:<flight>), none accepted; no view, no soft target, no trigger;
                       its own capture drops what its dispatch recorded and proposes nothing
  slots (161)          no player at take-2, no seat at take-1; the late enrollment above; the scope replays nothing until
                       H+delay (seated and neutral from H-1)
  bodies, foot slots   no body before its seat (its body after the settle ticks, as after a respawn); no walking (no seat)
  death (163)          not watched before its seat (its ship is nobody's); a loss before H stops at the seat (161)
  presentation (162)   on the joiner from H+3 (seat + take-to-start, as for the others), never before its handover
  fire                 its guns are not leased before its enrollment; no trigger before its first record
  UI replay (164)      no section accepted (ui_absent); the joiner's own UI events stay queued until H
  own prediction       nothing predicted or presented for it before H (reset, not counted)
  hit claims           its ship is nobody's: its hits stay native (as any ship's), it is no claim owner or PvP side
A missing record of a slot that is not absent at that tick still stops: 91 missing_record, 155 stock_missing_slot.
Lua: the UI replay core and the stock and hit sections stage only non-empty payloads (unchanged); the identity adapter has
no per-tick slot input (it sets up every slot at its tick, absent ones included); the stock and pilot captures accept
'absent'; the join core calls an optional abort(k).
Join pins (lockstep_join.h): the pinned functions are where the session's modules hook (lockstep_ai.h 106e3f6,
script_rng_md 112208b, md_resume_rng 1115bad, ...), so the live bytes never matched at T. Now: the pinned ranges are read
from X4.exe on disk and hashed against the pins (once, warmed at join_arm/join_load); at the save boundary and at the
joiner's admission every live byte that differs from the file must lie inside a hook of this gate (E8/E9 rel32, FF15/FF25
through a pointer) whose destination is the gate's DLL or a private page outside X4's image. Refusals name the place:
pins:<rva>, pins:file, patch:<rva> (JOIN_RESULT detail, JOIN_STATUS rx_detail); JOIN_STATUS shows pins= and
patched=<count>:<rvas>.
Offline checks: test_gate (--native-image): 'pilot inputs codec/...' with the absent block (no record through the window,
absent_record, absent capture, HANDOVER lead and slot checks, records required from H+delay, a missing slot-1 record
still missing_record, the join_inside_window stop); 'pilot stock slots: an absent slot (hot join) ...' (3 stock slots,
slot 3 unbuilt and unseated, S3:0:0 receipt, no view, slot 2 missing -> stock_missing_slot, built at H-2, seated at H-1,
replayed from H+delay, its first missing record -> stock_missing_slot, ABORT before and after the build, the package
state, a handover for take+1 built at take-1 and seated at take through the slots' own schedule); 'hot join native pins ...' (the five hook forms accepted, foreign bytes, jumps, pointers and file tampers refused).
pytest: test_native_gate_join*.py (overlay --join-absent, handoff 'absent', relay lead >= 3, the core's abort callback),
the defaults, registry, pilot, stock, foot, probe and inputs suites; the probe and the join core compile under LuaJIT.

9.2 The harness (offline-tested: tests/test_native_gate_join_driver.py, synthetic peers only)
- tools/dev/native_gate_join_overlays.py savers: the session overlay of N = 3 slots for a 2-lane session. The lane
  tool's @X4LANE_CLIENTS@ is its client count, which equals its lane count (session --clients must equal the number of
  --lanes, 2..3), so the recipe pins native_gate_clients = 3 in the overlay and the relay gets --clients 3 (not
  {clients}). The identity plan is built for 3 players (coop, stock, the session defaults: --defaults-only), with
  --join report|strict, native_gate_arm_timeout_ms = 120000 (the native gate timeout; the most the probe accepts) and
  the --set census keys passed on. joiner: the same recipe on the join save with the saver's package.
- tools/dev/native_gate_join_driver.py session: the session's --server-cmd. It starts the relay given after '--',
  passes its output through unchanged (--server-until/--server-fail-on work as before), sends NGJOIN for slot 3 once
  tick --join-at is released, polls NGJOINSTATE, and on 'saved' finds j<T>.xml.gz and j<T>.x4join in every saver's lane
  profile (lane b3 = profile 45853, b4 = 45854: Documents/Egosoft/X4/<profile>/save/mpjoin, the lane tool's
  X4LANE_PROFILES if set), verifies them (every hash and record shape, the SHA-256 each saver announced, the save the
  package names, the session's wire shape, the same portable content on both savers), copies slot 1's save and both
  packages to {outdir}/join/j<T>/ and writes {outdir}/join/handoff.json (the session's port and token, slot, wire
  shape, the copied files). Its UDP socket is bound to 127.0.0.1 (the lane tool fails a session whose server has any
  other endpoint).
- tools/dev/native_gate_lane.py --join-session HANDOFF -- run ...: the joiner as a lane run. The released lane tool has
  no 1-client session, and a run is offline (it refuses a config with a token and fills no session tokens), so the
  wrapper does in-process what it already does for the snapshot slot: the run's config is checked loopback-only (the
  released session-client check), the handoff's port, token, slot (p3) and N fill @X4LANE_PORT@, _TOKEN@, _SLOT@ and
  _CLIENT(S)@, and the handoff's save is staged as the snapshot. No release file changes.
- tools/dev/native_gate_join_driver.py replay: J1's relay for lane 3 after the session. It serves the joiner from the
  session's --input-journal (its first report must be T; game and real exact; the declared digest from T+1, every
  differing tick recorded with --observe; the permits the session itself sent) and stops it at the journal's end. It
  writes its own handoff (its port, a new token) and runs the joiner's lane command given after '--'.
- The token in handoff.json is the session's loopback secret: it stays under .local (as the lane tool's installed
  config copy does); never commit a handoff.

9.3 Commands (Git Bash, from the root of the checkout that has this branch; no other X4 than the lanes)
Set once (DLL and SNAP: the gate DLL and the owned snapshot save_009.xml.gz of today's census overlay):
  REPO="$PWD"; LT="$HOME/Documents/X4Mods/lanes/release-5d5a680ec6ef/dev/x4lane.py"; OUT="$REPO/.local/census"
  DLL=<x4lan_gate_64.dll of this build>; SNAP=<owned save_009.xml.gz>
  BASE=.local/lockstep-live/frozen-v3/src/extension/x4_lan_lab
  FAILON='NATIVE_GATE (ERROR (?!DEFAULT_SKIPPED|DEGRADED)|REFUSED)|NATIVE_GATE STOP .*reason=(?!1 )[0-9]|NATIVE_GATE JOIN_(ARM_)?REFUSED'
  (put the leading patterns of today's runner --fail-on in front of FAILON; '!' only inside single quotes, so an
  interactive shell's history expansion leaves the regexes alone.)

9.4 Fail-loud lines (each fails the session through --server-fail-on 'JOIN_DRIVER FAIL' or a client --fail-on)
  driver/relay start   usage errors on stderr (slot not absent, --saver-lanes, relay without --join): the lane tool
                       reports server_failed before any X4 starts
  saver arming         client 'NATIVE_GATE JOIN_ARM_REFUSED' / 'NATIVE_GATE JOIN_REFUSED' (FAILON); 'JOIN_RESULT failed|...'
  save refused         relay 'NATIVE_GATE JOIN slot=3 failed reason=<why>:slot_<S>' (not_possible, save_failed,
                       unsupported_module, capture, package_write, busy, timeout:no_saved_record, saver_disagree)
                       -> 'JOIN_DRIVER FAIL ... reason=join_failed:<why>'
  files                'JOIN_DRIVER FAIL ... reason=files:<why>' after --file-wait (60 s): missing_slot_<S>,
                       package_sha_slot_<S>, save_sha_slot_<S>, package_tick_or_slot_slot_<S>, wire_shape_slot_<S>_...,
                       portable_slot_<S>, savers_portable_content_differs, no_saved_notice_slots_<S>, copy_differs, os_*
  session first        'JOIN_DRIVER FAIL ... reason=session_done:state_<state>' (printed before the relay's done=True)
  joiner overlay       native_gate_join_overlays.py exits 2: package_sha_announced, save_sha, another N, a diagnostic
                       package with --join-mode strict, no handoff after --wait, saved IDs, fixture views,
                       lua_state:<provider>:<why> (1.5)
  joiner lane          native_gate_lane.py exits 2 on a handoff whose files changed; the probe asserts 'join package
                       refused ...' or 'join: the snapshot ... is not the package's save' (the run fails, X4 stops)
  admission            JOIN_ADMITTED counters (report mode continues; strict stops: JOIN_STOP and stop reason 200)
  probe restore (T+1)  'NATIVE_GATE ERROR ... join: ...' from a state provider (1.5: another or older fixture state, a session
                       wreck operational here, a live actor's gun missing, no world state, another pilot window or a T past
                       its take-2, the identity plan): the probe stops the gate (FAILON), no report of T+1
  catch-up             relay 'failed reason=mismatch:{game,real,digest}_tick_<t>' -> NGSTOP to the joiner and 'JOIN_DRIVER
                       FAIL' (J2); replay 'JOIN_REPLAY STOP reason=mismatch:...', or with --observe 'JOIN_REPLAY DESYNC'
                       and replay.json verdict diverged (J1)
  handover             'failed reason=pause_too_long:gap_<g>_rate_<r>' or 'joiner_lost' -> 'JOIN_DRIVER FAIL'
  A joiner stopped by the relay logs STOP reason=1 like a normal end, so its own lane verdict is not the join's: the
  driver's PASS/FAIL (J2) and replay.json (J1) are.

9.5 Measured by the harness: JOIN_DRIVER PAUSE is the longest gap between two released ticks within T-2..T+k+30 (the
  save) or H-2..H+k+30 (the final pause), as the driver read the relay's tick lines; the catch-up rate and time are the
  relay's (joiner_rate, catch_up_s); JOIN_REPLAY DONE steps_per_s is the J1 joiner's replay speed.

9.6 What the lane tool and the current build cannot do (stated, not worked around)
  - Lane client count: session --clients 2..3 equals the lane count and is what @X4LANE_CLIENTS@ becomes; a 3-slot
    session on 2 lanes therefore pins native_gate_clients = 3 in the overlay and runs the relay with --clients 3.
  - No 1-client session and an offline-only run: the joiner is a run made loopback by native_gate_lane.py
    --join-session (9.2). The driver cannot start lane 3 itself: the lane tool keeps the server and everything it starts
    in a kill-on-close job and fails the session on any non-loopback endpoint (X4's Steam connection would be one), so
    lane 3 comes from a second terminal (J2) or from the replay's command (J1).
  - J1 d's save_diff of a lane 3 save: a joiner never saves (the join core applies no SAVE_AT on a joiner and the
    relay's mask names the savers) and the probe has no other save trigger; census captures and the replay's per-tick
    digest check stand in. --join-at takes a list, so a second session save is possible once the first join ended
    (relay --join-joiner-timeout small), but that compares the savers, not lane 3.
  - Strict J2: a strict saver refuses while any unaudited or unsupported module is armed, and the default stack arms
    many (lockstep_join.h join modules: script_rng, md_timed_rng, event_order, mass_traffic, nav_* and more are
    unaudited), so strict savers answer FAILED unsupported_module at T, and a strict joiner refuses the diagnostic
    package of report savers. J2 runs report/report until those modules are classified; strict is then the same
    commands with --join strict and --join-mode strict.
  - Slot 3's inputs after H: the recipe is the people session (--defaults-only), which refuses --stock-script; an
    unattended J2 shows the handover and equality, slot 3 acts only through a person at lane b5's window.
  - Pauses: native_gate_arm_timeout_ms is at most 120 s, so --join-max-pause stays below it (100).

  loader shape at selector 0: cursor 1, both banks one rounded state, mode 27; after the admission world_nav_capture and
  world_nav_encode give the session's timing and motion bytes; the attention levels 5/3 over 2 and the ship's due; the
  attention_all, parameter-init and actor keys; refusals nav_owner_invalid at capture, nav_shape, keys_unarmed, keys_actor,
  created_extra with loaded=1, health_conflict with the shield groups agreeing, version 2 placing HEALTH and POSE only), the
  native pins with the four new ranges and their layout bytes on X4.exe [M]. pytest: test_native_gate_join.py (v3 golden
  shared with C, v2 and v1 still read, every new section's shape, order and digest; the savers' comparison names NAV, ATTN
  and KEYS records), the overlay's world text and v2 refusal (test_native_gate_join_driver.py).
